`need: true` on their block definitions) and a real check box *Needed* at the top of each of those blocks' own page. **(2) Pre-ticked from the Fact Find** (`store.blocksAsked`, written as `factFind.facts.asked.blocks`): the BrightRock FNA (`bf_fna_req`), shortfall (`bf_sf_req`) and each `bfred` row by their own type (Servicing: the servicing block; New Business, N/A or empty: the new business block), the standard lane (`fna_req`, `sf_req`, a `redq` row) as Comparative quotes, `raq` / `invq` rows as Proposed investments; Straight increase and Straight reduction never. **(3) The adviser's tick wins** (`blocks.<id>.needed`, written by `store.setNeeded` only when he ticks or unticks; kept when a file opens (`fromJSON` rebuilt blocks from their quotes and items only, so it was lost until my own Save-and-Open test found it); a later read of the Fact Find never overrides it); `calc.needed(state, id)` = his tick, else the Fact Find's, else `{ on: null }` (no Fact Find read: Load looks for everything, as before). **(4) Load looks only for the needed quotes** (`tickPlan`'s `notAsked` map; a pay-out guide goes with its quote; a bare Serv quote is asked for when either straight line is) **and lists every other quote once, collapsed, as *Found but not asked for (n)*, unticked, with its reason** (open, with a sentence, when no line is needed at all); a quote read into a block that was not needed ticks it (the block it landed in: a bare Serv quote lands in increase or reduction by its premium). **(5) The menu reads as a checklist** (`calc.checklist`): *Asked for · outstanding*, *Asked for · loaded (n)*, *Loaded (n) · not asked for*, *Not asked for*, and under the Servicing heading *Quotes: asked for a · loaded b · outstanding c*; **a quote nobody asked for is dashed and says *Not asked for*, never red (replaces 0.9's red *No shortfall quote requested* / *No comparative quotes* and 2.0's *Not requested*)**; an asked-for line is red only once the folder was searched and nothing was found; Back and Next pass a dashed page by; a Fact Find proposed row counts as loaded only once a document or the adviser filled it; a shortfall line with no group risk says *Not needed: no group risk* and is neither asked for nor outstanding; New review year does not carry last year's `asked.blocks` or ticks. **Version 2.2 (the decimal after 2.1); contract revision q: TWO optional keys (`blocks.<id>.needed`, `factFind.facts.asked.blocks`); and a bare quote is asked for when either line is); (b) Proposed investments read *loaded* before anything was loaded (the Fact Find's own row was counted); (c) New Review year carried last year's `asked.blocks`; a *Not needed* progress bar outlived a test's wait (no bar now); the *Not asked for* comment was drawn under the same words of the checklist line (drawn once); my first group count in a test said six where seven quotes were found. **My decisions, for Paul to correct:** Proposed investments has a tick box (investment quote requests had no other home) and Goal analysis reports none; a type that is N/A or empty is new business; the standard lane is Comparative quotes; a quote nobody asked for is dashed, never red; with no Fact Find read everything is looked for; loading a quote ticks its box; a comparative quote in a policy folder's review folder is listed in the collapsed group unless the Fact Find asked for a comparative quote or Paul ticks the line. **Not certain:** seen only in my own screenshots with an invented client; the pre-tick has never run on a real Fact Find; an older overview shows no ticks until the Fact Find is read again; on a client whose request boxes are blank every quote goes to the open group, unticked (told to Paul). **After "merge":** the routine (section 4), the ZIP, and the FOR THE FIX SKILLS CHAT block (contract revision q: not display only). |

for a quote block (`kind === 'quotes'`: the five servicing blocks, fna, shortfallQuote, reduced, comparative) only with a quote; proposed and goals unchanged (my reading: Paul named only the quote pages). The PDF's pages and tab row, the Presentation's tabs (`pres.blockOn`) follow; an empty page says so in ONE line (PDF: *There are no current policies in place.*, *No medical aid, gap cover, will, legacy plan or short-term insurance is recorded.* new, group and investments as before, shortfall *There is no FNA quote yet...*; the Presentation: policies and investments reworded; the Presentation's Other page keeps its card for each cover, each *not discussed*). The Fact Find asking for a shortfall or comparative quote no longer prints an empty page; an empty FNA or Reduced page is gone. **(2) The naming guide** (`docs.classify`): `SERV_NAMED` sends *Serv FNA / Serv Shortfall / Serv Reduced / Serv Increase / Serv Decrease* (also Servicing, Service, Reduction) to its servicing block WHEREVER the file sits; `BR Quote` is a comparative quote; a `signed` flag; the five servicing blocks joined `REVIEW_KINDS` so a signed copy in a Review or Servicing folder stays paperwork; `FF.folders.signedTwins` (pure; `tickPlan` calls it): a signed copy is ticked only when it is the ONLY copy of its quote, else unticked with its reason; BrightRock files in it: *<Name>'s cover terms (Cover Proposal <original> <revised>).pdf* = the counter offer LETTER (5 pages); *<Name>'s Cover Proposal <revised>.pdf* = the counter offer QUOTE (a full Cover Proposal, about 29 pages); *Consent to release confidential information (Cover Proposal <n>).pdf* = ignore; a copy ending *- signed.pdf* = the client's signed acceptance (a sign the client accepted). Older Liberty / Discovery / Old Mutual files have free names. (2) THE LETTER'S WORDING: pages 1 and 2: original premium, revised premium, the percentage change with the rand amount in brackets, the original and revised Cover Proposal numbers, the revised policy start date, *Revised Cover Proposal expires on <date>*. Page 3 *your revised underwriting terms*: a GRID; rows = need groups (*household, childcare, healthcare, debt and death-related needs*; *additional expense needs*); column groups = BrightRock's three: *that you can recover from*, *that's permanent*, *that's caused by death*; under each: Decision, Additional charge (*Premium loading n%*), Exclusions (*Yes* and a number and an asterisk), Reduced cover. **So a loading belongs to a need group AND a column group and can differ between them (the percentages can differ); the grid prints no reason.** Page 4 *Cover exclusions*: number, type (for example Medical), *what it means* (a paragraph). Page 5: lump-sum values per column. (3) THE COUNTER OFFER QUOTE PRINTS THE LOADED PREMIUM (*Total premium (including the policy fee)*), a cent different from the letter's (rounding), and has its own Additional charge / Exclusions grid and exclusions table. **Their suggestion: the letter is the source of the terms and of the before / after premium; the revised Cover Proposal is the source of the lines and the per-benefit amounts.** (4) **The mapping of BrightRock's three column groups to Life cover / Severe illness / Lump sum disability / Income protector needs Paul's confirmation: I was asked to ask him (my guess: caused by death = Life cover; permanent = Lump sum disability; can recover from = Severe illness and Income protector).** Paul still has to answer my three questions himself (the Overview-card sentences; *Counter offers* as a PDF page and Presentation tab; whether the red *will be replaced by* note goes when a counter offer is not accepted). **What this changed in my plan (told to Paul in a short table; then built as 1.2):** the pick list reads the exact folder *Counter Offer Letter* of the policy folder that holds the accepted quote (found as the Comparative Quotes folder is: `clientDocuments` in `folders.js`; name matched ignoring capitals and extra spaces); the file role comes from the name (letter / quote / consent ignored / *- signed* copy), the signed copy is only a hint shown to the adviser, never the client's decision; a loading and an exclusion row carry the need group, the column group, the insurer's number, type, wording and page (no per-line key: the red references are derived from them); the loading's reason stays empty and amber (the grid does not print it); the letter's expiry date is shown. **Before it:** **UPDATE from the Fix Skills chat (relayed 24 November 17:52, "Studio 1.1 handled"; recorded here without a pull request): (1) `client-overview-skill` was renewed for contract revision m: **20261124-1744**. ## 1. What this is and who uses it

| Item | What it is |
|---|---|
| **The WR Fact Find** | A static web page (no server, no install, no build) that Paul Kafentzis, a financial adviser at Wealth Revolution (FSP45946), fills in with a client, by typing or by voice. It makes a saved file (.json) and a PDF. |
| **Who uses it** | **Paul** (adviser) in meetings; **Sitamah Nathoo** (his personal assistant) reads the action points and does the follow-up; the **skills** (see below) read and fill the saved file. |
| **Where it runs** | **The website** https://pavlos1972.github.io/Fill_In-Form_Project/ (GitHub Pages, published from `main`), and **an offline copy** on Paul's computer: OneDrive `Company - Paul\Claude\Fact Find Documents\WR Fact Find - ZIP\WR Fact Find`, installed by the Fix Skills chat from the ZIP. |
| **Four pages** (the fourth, the **Client Overview** `app/overview.html`, is new in 5.4: see section 0 and `docs/OVERVIEW-DATA-CONTRACT.md`) | The Fact Find (`app/index.html`); the **Risk Application** (`app/short-risk.html`, fills from a Fact Find file; **called the Short Risk Application until 1.3, and still "Short Risk" in file names, ids, the `app` key `wr-short-risk` and this file's older rows**); the **Investment Application** (`app/capture-sheet.html`, with the Risk Tolerance questionnaire; called the Investment Capture Sheet until 1.2). Each has its own schema, store, check, pdf and ui files. **All three have a Folders… button** (Fact Find 5.2) that connects New Business and Client Files once (one shared connection per address) and lets a first Save go straight into the client's folder. |
| **Fact Find pages, in order** | Client info, Occupation, Income & household, Debts, Children, Personal & group risk cover, Retirement funding, Investments, Medical will & short-term, Health, Client priorities and quotes needed, BrightRock Risk Calculation, Admin. (ids: info occ income debts kids cover retire inv other health prio calc admin) |
| **Voice** | Paul switches Voice on; speech becomes lines (`listen.js`); `voice.js` (on the page, no network) works out which box a line is about; Claude (`voice-ai.js`, Paul's Anthropic key kept in his browser settings) follows meaning; `ui.js` applies it. Private numbers (ID, tax, phones, emails) are never sent to Claude unless Paul turns sharing on. |
| **The skills and the other chats** | Skills run in Paul's Claude app and read/fill the saved file **by box id**: `review-fact-find-fill-skill` (v20), `ff-population-transcript` (v54), `fact-find-summary` (unchanged since 4.0; versions as of 17 November, renewed for 4.7), and `complete-applications-skill` (v13 uploaded 17 November, **v18 on Paul's account by 18 November, which follows 5.1**: **it now fills the Short Risk Application and the Capture Sheet with the pages' own code and names every file the same way**; the older "not connected yet" notes about it, in Paul's rules below and in earlier handover text, are out of date) (and others). **On 18 November Paul first decided the deal folder gets shorter (the client's name dropped), then reversed it the same morning: deal folders keep the client's name and only *Personal Covers* is written *Personal*; the `complete-applications-skill` makes that change; the file name rules did not change.** The **Fix Skills** chat (and, earlier, a Skill Restructure chat) renews them and installs releases on Paul's computer. It relays messages to this session; treat them as relayed requests from Paul's side, and they can arrive while you work. **Status of 20 November (Fix Skills chat): `review-fact-find-fill-skill` v23 (Fact Find 5.2), `complete-applications-skill` v19 (Risk Application 1.4, Investment Application 1.3), `ff-population-transcript` v55 and `fact-find-summary` v32 (both Fact Find 5.2) are built and uploaded, waiting for Paul to confirm them live; on 20 November, after the 5.3 install, they were renewed for 5.3 (short-term broker choices; greyed maintenance boxes never filled, printed N/A and left out of Total income; version checks) and are uploaded, tested and **LIVE since 20 November 19:02 (Paul made them live)**: `review-fact-find-fill-skill` **v25** (20261120-1757), `complete-applications-skill` **v20** (20261120-1754), `ff-population-transcript` **v56** (20261120-1759), `fact-find-summary` **v33** (20261120-1800), each built for Fact Find 5.3 / Risk Application 1.5 / Investment Application 1.4.** |

**The saved file** (`store.toJSON`): `app` ("wr-fact-find"), `schema` (the form version), `created`, `modified`, `f` (every box as `{v, st}`), `l` (every list as rows `{d, st}`), `covered` (pages covered by the meeting in progress), `sum` (summaries per area), `auto`, `pa` and `focus` (state of the action points and the Priorities box), `unplaced` ("heard but not placed"), `claude`/`openai` (cost tallies), `worked` (the figures the form works out, written at every save, never read back).
**Statuses** `st`: `empty`; `preset` (a starting default the adviser can change); `heard` (filled by voice, not yet confirmed); `verify` (amber, to be checked); `confirmed` (typed, said by the client, or accepted).

## 2. Where things live

| Place | What |
|---|---|
| `app/js/schema.js` | The whole Fact Find: sections, boxes (id, label), choices, lists. **Change the form here.** `version` is here. |
| `calc.js`, `tax.js`, `validate.js`, `autoprio.js` | Calculated boxes and show/hide rules; SARS tables (update each tax year); the Checks; automatic "Not a priority" and the suggestion lines |
| `store.js` | Answers, saving, reopening, file names, migrations of older files, defaults that follow other answers |
| `actions.js`, `summary.js`, `worked.js` | Action points for Sitamah (facts from the form, plain points); the area summaries; the `worked` block |
| `pdf.js`, `ui.js`, `util.js`, `build.js` | The PDF (drawn from the schema); the screen; helpers; the copy check (build number and the red "Mixed versions" bar) |
| `voice.js`, `voice-ai.js`, `listen.js`, `lookup.js`, `timer.js` | Voice (page side, Claude side, speech source), company look-up and keys, meeting timer |
| `sr-*.js`, `cs-*.js` | The Risk Application and the Investment Application (called the Short Risk Application and the Investment Capture Sheet until 1.3 / 1.2; schema, store, check, pdf, ui; `cs-rt-*` is the questionnaire) |
| `folders.js`, `folders-ui.js`, `css/folders.css` | Saving straight into the client's OneDrive folder (the deal folder's Applications folder for the two applications, the client's `2. Fact Find` folder for the Fact Find), shared by the three pages |
| `ov-schema.js`, `ov-calc.js`, `ov-store.js`, `ov-check.js`, `ov-docs.js`, `ov-pdf.js`, `ov-pres.js`, `ov-present.js`, `ov-view.js` (0.95: the View buttons and the document window), `ov-ui.js`, `css/overview.css`, `css/present.css`, `css/view.css`, `overview.html` | (5.4) The Client Overview page: the blocks, pillars and file name; the figures the page works out (totals, shortfall, comparison); the data (saved file, typing, quotes and lines, pre-fill from the Fact Find); the checks and the dots; the screen. The layout of its saved file is `docs/OVERVIEW-DATA-CONTRACT.md` |
| `logkeep.js` | (5.3) The conversation log kept in the browser as it is heard (IndexedDB; an in-memory twin for the tests); used by `ui.js` (Fact Find only) |
| `handover.js` | (5.3) Moving between the three pages: the Fact Find's client left in localStorage for an hour for the application that opens next, `sameClient`, the `?from=factfind` flag, and the Other forms menu's closing (all three pages) |
| `tests/*.test.js` | `npm test` (Node 20+; 1,237 tests and the browser suite (2,329 checks) at Fact Find 5.5 / Client Overview Studio 2.2; includes `readme.test.js`, `pdf-mirror.test.js`, `worked.test.js`) |
| `tests/browser/` | Playwright checks on the real page: `bash tests/browser/run.sh <outdir>` (or `npm run test:browser`); afterwards `git checkout -- tests/browser/*.png`. Five slow scripts can time out when all run together and pass alone. |
| `.github/workflows/` | `tests.yml` (tests on every pull request); `pages.yml` (tests, then publishes `app/` to the website when `main` changes; can also be started by hand) |
| `docs/FORM-CHANGES.md` | The dated change log of ids and lists, newest first |
| `docs/OVERVIEW-DATA-CONTRACT.md` | **The Client Overview's data contract (stage 1, version 0.1):** the saved file's layout, the fixed pillars, columns, line types and blocks, what the page computes, the checks, the pre-fill map from the Fact Find, what the filling skill writes; invented examples only |

| The ZIP | The whole app folder as one ZIP: `git archive --format=zip --prefix="WR Fact Find/" -o "WR Fact Find - Version X.Y.zip" HEAD:app` |
| The changes page | An Artifact: https://claude.ai/artifact/TG8Jqe8ndzoZKUuuZhUwpP (Paul's list of what changed, per release; at version 76 it covers 4.9 / PR 84 to Client Overview Studio 1.9 / PR 119 and the quote numbers clean-up / PR 98 and PR 99, the names clean-up and the Support request, updated on Paul's word on 18, 19, 20, 22 and 23 November; the working copy lived in Chat 2's scratchpad and is gone: rebuild it from the live page, `docs/HANDOVER-TO-CHAT-3.md` section 6) |

GitHub: use the `mcp__github__*` tools (no `gh` CLI). Repository `pavlos1972/fill_in-form_project`. Work branch: **Chat 1 used `claude/create-something-stjk8a` and Chat 2 `claude/gallant-turing-hoe5mi`; Chat 3 works and pushes only on the branch its own session gives it** (the session sets it; everywhere this file or the handover says "the work branch" it means the one the current session was given). Never put real client data in tests, commits or the README: use invented names.

## 3. Paul's standing rules

| Rule | In practice |
|---|---|
| **Nothing is merged until Paul says "merge"** | Build, test, push to the work branch, tell Paul it is ready and what changed. Wait. Do not open a pull request unless he asks. |
| **Merge only the exact commit the Tests run passed on** | Take the SHA from `git rev-parse HEAD`, never from memory; check the Tests job for that commit is `success`; squash-merge with `expectedHeadSha`. |
| **Versions are compared as decimals** | The code does `parseFloat(schema)`: so the next number after 3.9 is **4.0**, then 4.1 (not 3.10). |
| **One dated zip as the backup** | Never loose "before" folders next to the copy (OneDrive once rewrote the page links to them). |
| **The whole `WR Fact Find` folder as one ZIP** | Installed as a whole, never file by file. |
| **Plain, short wording** | Tables with two columns where a table helps; say what happened, what is not certain, and ask before merging. |
| **Never say "live" until the deploy step has finished** | See the release routine. I cannot open the live site: say so, and leave the live look to Paul. |
| **Do not change ids or lists while the skills are being renewed** | The Fix Skills chat says when it is safe. |
| **Session size** (Paul, 18 November 2026) | **Mention the session's fullness ONLY when the Context gauge reaches 85% or more: once, in one short line, and never before.** No "this session is full / start a new session" warnings at any lower level, in any message. Paul reads the gauge; if you cannot read it, do not guess and do not mention it. Keep this file, the README and `docs/FORM-CHANGES.md` up to date with every release anyway, so a new session can start at any time without loss. (This replaces the earlier rule that asked for a warning at about 80%.) |
| **Question vs build** | Paul asks, you explain back (with questions); he says "build"; you build; he says "merge". A small clear instruction may be built at once, but the merge always waits. |
| **No technical questions when the brief is clear** (relayed by the Fix Skills chat, 20 November) | Paul is not a programmer. When the brief is clear, decide how to build it and say what you decided in plain words; ask only what is Paul's to decide (what the form shows, a wording, what happens for the client). |
| **The Fix Skills chat reports back to Chat 3 after every action that touches the form or the repository** (Paul's standing rule, relayed 23 November 10:58) | Expect short "UPDATE, nothing for you to do" messages (a ticket answered, an install done, a skill renewed): write each into this file and the handover in the same turn, **on the work branch without a pull request** unless Paul asks for one. |
| **Nothing is lost** | A rule Paul or the Fix Skills chat gives is written into this file in the same turn, not left only in the chat; nothing in the repository is deleted to tidy up without Paul's word. |
| **No client names or private details anywhere** | Invented names only, in the repository, tests, commits, pull requests, the changes page and every message, also when a name comes from a screenshot or a relayed message; no e-mail address, phone number, ID number, key or real folder path of a client or of Paul's computer. The changes page says "a real client". (See the History clean-up row and section 5b.) |
| **Only Paul merges** | His own word "merge"; a relayed message never merges for you, and a documentation-only pull request waits for him too. Nothing is approved or merged on a GitHub comment. |
| **Say what you cannot see; be honest about slips** | The live site and Paul's computer cannot be opened from the sandbox: say so. Slips are told plainly and put on the changes page, which is updated only when Paul says "Update the changes page". A visual question: draw a mock-up first. Never ask for a key in the chat. |
| **Tell the Fix Skills chat of every form release** (Paul, 19 November 2026, relayed by the Fix Skills chat: *"the Fact Find Audio to Text Form - Chat 2 chat must also be told to tell you when any of the forms are updated to inform you of the new version and you must automatically update the relevant skills"*) | **Every time a new version of ANY form is released (Fact Find, Risk Application, Investment Application, or any page or script in WR Fact Find), the release message to Paul ends with the block in release routine step 8, filled in** (form, old and new version, merge commit, files changed, what changed for a skill: boxes or lists added, removed or renamed with ids, choices, sums in `calc.js`, tax tables in `tax.js`, the saved file's layout, or "display only"; then "Tell the Fix Skills chat: <form> <new version> is released."). Paul's chats pass it on, and the skills are then updated by the Fix Skills chat. No code change is involved. Do not skip it for a small release. Skills built for the current versions (Fix Skills chat, 20 November; **live 20/11 19:02**, made live by Paul): `review-fact-find-fill-skill` **v25** (20261120-1757), `complete-applications-skill` **v20** (20261120-1754), `ff-population-transcript` **v56** (20261120-1759), `fact-find-summary` **v33** (20261120-1800), each for Fact Find 5.3 / Risk Application 1.5 / Investment Application 1.4 (before them: v23, v19, v55 and v32 for 5.2 / 1.4 / 1.3). |

## 4. The release routine (every release)

1. **Build on the work branch.** Bump `version` in `app/js/schema.js` and `BUILD` in `app/js/build.js`, the `wr-build` meta tag in the three pages, the version pins in the tests (`grep "schema.version"` in `tests/`), and the `?v=` tags of the changed scripts and `app.css` in the pages.
2. **Write the records in the same commit:** README (a "Release X.Y" section, and correct every statement the change makes untrue), `docs/FORM-CHANGES.md`, the PDF (it is drawn from the schema; hand-drawn parts by hand), `worked.js` if a figure changed.
3. **Tests:** `npm test`, then `bash tests/browser/run.sh <dir>` (the PDF check `audit-pdf` must show only the known items: public profile, Current Client notes). Add a unit test file and a browser test for the release (`tests/release-NN.test.js`, `tests/browser/t-releaseNN.js`, added to the `SIMPLE` list in `run.sh`).
4. **Commit and push** to the work branch; tell Paul what is built and what is not certain; ask "merge?".
5. **On "merge":** open the pull request; wait for the Tests job on that exact commit; squash-merge with `expectedHeadSha`; then `git fetch origin main && git merge -s ours origin/main` on the work branch and push (resync).
6. **Publish check:** the "Publish the Fact Find" run on `main` must finish with the **deploy step** `success` (the status call can lag: ask again). Only then say it is live. If the deploy step fails with "no artifact" or "multiple artifacts", **re-run failed jobs once at most**; a re-run of the same run adds another website package and then fails for good. Start a **fresh run** (`run_workflow`, `pages.yml`, ref `main`) instead. Tell Paul plainly if it is still not through.
7. **ZIP:** build it (command in section 2), check it holds the new `build.js` build number, send it to Paul.
8. **Note for the Fix Skills chat, at the end of every release (Paul's standing rule of 19 November, relayed by the Fix Skills chat: the skills must be updated automatically when a form changes): end the release message to Paul with this block, filled in, for EVERY release of ANY form, page or script (Fact Find, Risk Application, Investment Application): "FOR THE FIX SKILLS CHAT - FORM RELEASE: <form> <old version> -> <new version>, merged as <commit>; files changed: <list>; what changed for a skill: <boxes or lists added / removed / renamed (with ids), choices changed, sums (calc.js) or tax tables (tax.js) changed, the saved file's layout (store.js keys, schema stamp, open-time conversions)>, or 'display only'. Tell the Fix Skills chat: <form> <new version> is released.". It carries:** the version; every new, changed or removed box id and list; anything a skill that **reads or writes the saved file** must know (a value now empty, a new top-level key, a changed meaning); anything that would make a skill's form check stop (a new id or list it does not know). The skills are renewed from it.
9. **Wait for the Fix Skills chat to confirm the skills are renewed before the release is installed on Paul's computer.** **The Fix Skills chat installs the release on Paul's computer itself** (only the changed files, read back against `main`) and keeps one dated zip in Fact Find Documents; Paul does not need to install it by hand. (Still send Paul the ZIP, as a copy.)
10. **The changes page** (when Paul asks): read it with the Artifact tool (`read`), add a section for the release and update the status line (PR, commit, test counts, deploy time), then publish to the same URL. Be honest in it about slips and what is not certain.
11. **A documentation-only pull request** (for example the one that carries a handover) is done the same way from step 4 on, with no version bump, no ZIP, no website update (`pages.yml` runs only for `app/**`) and no block for the Fix Skills chat unless a form changed; Paul still merges it. **The routine with the tool names, the commands and the traps is in `docs/HANDOVER-TO-CHAT-3.md` section 4.**

## 5. Key decisions and why

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **One ACI for every benefit** (4.0) | BrightRock's ACI is the same across a policy's benefits. A value entered in any of the four fractured ACI boxes (`lc_f_aciv`, `pd_f_aciv`, `ip_aciv`, `dd_aciv`) goes into the others; while a BrightRock policy is listed `br_aci` takes part. ABI, the ACI choice and the standard quotes are not part of it. |
| **`br_aci` is BrightRock's Yearly premium increase** (3.9) | On the BR Cover Summary (page 2) the Yearly premium increase is the ACI (10.0% on the example) and the Yearly base increase is the ABI (3.0%). The first build used the base increase by mistake; Paul corrected it. The ABI is never filled from the policy. |
| **Dread** (3.9) | Fractured Dread cover = the greater of R 500 000 and `br_dd_cover` (the cover "that you can recover from"). |
| **No "?"** (4.0) | The ACI value boxes start empty; a "?" is an empty box; older files open with it empty or filled from the policy; the Checks flag an empty ACI value once a quote is asked for. |
| **Debts and the payer** (3.6) | Only debts the client pays come off take-home pay: Payer Client, Joint (his half) or blank. `debtSplit` in `store.js`: Joint splits the instalment 50/50 into `inst` and `sinst` (Spouse instalment); Spouse puts it all in `sinst`. "Total spouse monthly" is shown apart. Credit life is asked only for a Home loan, Vehicle finance or Personal loan with a lender, with Yes / No / Not sure. |
| **Has debts = No gives R 0.00** (3.9) | The debt-repayment boxes show R 0.00 and "Client has no debts"; empty while Has debts is unanswered. |
| **Meetings** (4.0) | `meetings` is empty while there has been one meeting. "Add another meeting" makes the meeting so far meeting 1. **Date completed, Type of meeting and Others on the call are the last meeting's.** "What was covered" is written by the form (page names where an answer was typed or said), from `covered`. Opening a saved file on a later day with voice on asks "another meeting?". |
| **Appointments** (4.1) | When `next_appt` has passed, Admin asks what happened (Completed, Did not meet with the client, Cancelled by the client, Rescheduled). The answer goes to `appts`; `next_appt` and `next_time` are emptied. Completed does not add a meeting. |
| **Action points** (3.8) | Written by Claude from the facts; **every point must name its source** (a kind of fact, or a note id with words quoted from it) or it is removed; what the client or adviser *will* do is information, not a task; stale "heard but not placed" items the form covers are closed. The Admin page lists the notes read and the points removed. |
| **Priorities and autoprio** | The one "no" is plain **Not a priority** (the reason goes in the Comments). `autoprio.js` sets it by itself only where a fact makes the area pointless (medical aid through the company, a will up to date elsewhere, short-term through the company, no children); a cover with another broker is "an opportunity to quote; the client will decide", never "a take-over". The Priorities box is Recommend / Client / Come back with. |
| **Phones** (4.1) | `cell` is labelled **Personal cell number** (the id did not change: ids never change when only a label does); `work_cell` is new; Work number and Home number are the landlines. The Short Risk and Capture Sheet pages copy `cell` and `work`. |
| **The BrightRock Risk Calculation** | Only how a BrightRock Fractured quote is built; standard quotes are on Client priorities. Starting answers are presets. The instalment protector is split out of the income-protection amount. |
| **Keys and costs table** (4.3) | Paul found the top right muddled; he chose "Option B" from a mock-up page and asked for "Anthropic" instead of "Claude". One table: a line per service, columns Key and This Fact Find; the two page buttons stay above it. When a design question is visual, **draw a mock-up Artifact first** and let Paul choose. Since 4.4 the two page links are one "Other forms ▾" menu (the mix of Option B and Option A that Paul asked for). |
| **The copy check** (3.8) | Each page checks its files all come from its own folder and that its build matches; otherwise a red bar says "Mixed versions". |
| **Voice never fills** `meetings`, `appts` | They are the adviser's records (`novoice` in `schema.js`). |
| **Gap cover needs a medical aid** (4.6) | Paul: *"where the Medical Aid = NO - the Gap Cover must be greyed out - you can't have a Gap Cover without a Medical Aid"*. `gap`, `gap_prov` and `gap_broker` are greyed (not hidden, nothing cleared) while `ma` is None; an unanswered `ma` leaves them open; the Gap notes box stays open; the untouched Other broker default under Gap goes while `ma` is None (`BROKER_OF` in `store.js` has a second gate); the Checks flag a Yes / Not sure, provider or chosen broker left under it (not a plain No); the summary and action points ignore it. This replaced the 3.6-era test that let a client with no medical aid have gap cover with another broker. |
| **High blood sugar, not diabetes** (4.7) | Paul (via the Fix Skills chat): *"Maybe we can just say 'High blood sugar', remove diabetes"*: `h_dia` is only the client's raised blood sugar; **diabetes is a diagnosis the form does not record** (it goes in Other health issues and the Health notes). The label, the voice words (no "diabetes" or "diabetic") and the PDF follow; the id and the Yes / No never change, so every older file opens as it was. Do not put the word back in the label, a voice word or a prompt for this box without Paul's word. **The voice words stay as built (no "diabetes")** unless Paul says otherwise (Fix Skills chat, 17 November). The skills were renewed for it: `ff-population-transcript` v54 says "High blood sugar" for `h_dia` (and its list of greyed boxes is regenerated from the 4.7 schema), `review-fact-find-fill-skill` v20 reads "High blood sugar" into `h_dia` and still reads the old "Diabetes / High Sugar" row of older Transcripts into the same box, `fact-find-summary` needed no change; no skill prints "diabetes" for `h_dia`. |
| **Headings in the Priorities box and the Action Points** (4.8) | Paul: the headings (*"1. Personal Risk Covers"*, *"Sitamah, please action these for the client Elmer Jonker:"*) *"need to be a font larger and in bold ... with proper spacing between the heading and the points"*. A text box cannot show bold, so both boxes use the read view the Current Client notes have (field `heads`: `1` capitals, `'focus'` numbered area titles, `'pa'` the opening line; `util.headingKind`, `util.headingBig`); the text in the file stays plain. The PDF prints the same headings larger and bold. Do not make these boxes rich text or put markup in the file. |
| **Investment files are named like the Short Risk files** (Capture Sheet 1.1) | Paul (via the Fix Skills chat, 17 November): *"I recommend investments follow - agreed"*. The name is `<Insurer> - <Product> Application - dd.mm.yy - <R amount p.m. or R amount - Lump Sum> - (First Last)`. Product: Old Mutual's own product name first (Optimal RA, Tax-Free, Flexi, Xtra Max RA, Wealth RA), otherwise the Product type (RA, Tax-Free, Endowment, Living Annuity; Other is the Product typed, else Other). Missing parts read as what they are (`Provider`, `Product`, `R amount`, `(New client)`). **The short names are one table, `fileName` in `cs-schema.js`: change a name there, and the skill reads the same table.** A file that is opened keeps its own name when saved again (the existing rule). A change to this page does not bump the Fact Find's version (as with Capture Sheet 1.0): only `cs-schema.js`' `version` and the `?v=` tags of the changed scripts. |
| **The "heard" tag** (4.6) | The microphone line under the top bar and the tag on a box both carry the class `heard`; the microphone line's rules are `div.heard` so the tag (a `button`) is never caught. Do not write a bare `.heard` rule. |
| **A saved file is proved to be saved, and a failed save says the truth** (4.9, corrected in 5.0) | Paul's screenshots (Short Risk Application): *"Could not save: A requested file or directory could not be found at the time an operation was processed"*, and PDFs in the folder that Acrobat called damaged. **4.9 blamed OneDrive and retried: that was WRONG** (it failed again on 18 November, OneDrive fully synced, and emptied a good file). **Measured on Paul's computer by the Fix Skills chat: it is the Windows path limit.** Chrome writes `<file>.crswap` (7 characters longer) beside the file; when the folders + file name + `.crswap` exceed 259 characters, close() fails with `NotFoundError` every time and the file in the folder is left EMPTY (160 + 94 = 254, +7 = 261 failed; 258 saved). Fact Find names are short (about 68), Short Risk about 101, Capture Sheet up to about 100: so the Fact Find never failed. **Lesson: measure before blaming; I inferred a cause I could not test.** **Rules (do not undo):** `util.writeVerified` (`util.js`) is the only way the three pages write into the folder: read back (size equal, `%PDF-` for a PDF); **`NotFoundError` is never retried**; any other error is tried three times (pauses 0.8 s and 2 s; `util.WRITE_PAUSES`); Cancel and a refused permission are never retried. `util.saveFailText` says, for NotFoundError or a 0-byte read-back, *Windows could not save <file>: the folder path and file name together are too long (Windows allows 260 characters), or the folder was moved or renamed ... a copy is in your Downloads folder ... Ask Claude to put it in the client's folder, or save it in a folder with a shorter name. The file in the folder may now be EMPTY: do not use it.* **Never blame OneDrive for NotFoundError**; the OneDrive wording is only for other errors. A failed Save counts as unsaved (dirty, draft kept, failed file let go). **Paul's first decision (18 November) was a shorter deal folder without the client's name; he reversed it the same morning (see section 0).** **The file name rule does NOT change.** Keep the whole path under 259 characters including `.crswap` (the page cannot know the folder path, so it cannot warn before saving). The folder that failed was shaped `<the adviser's OneDrive path>\New Business\<Surname, First>\<Surname, First> - (<Insurer> - <Product> - <R amount p.m.>)\Applications` (160 characters for a long surname; the real path is deliberately not written here). **Paul's later decision (18 November, relayed): the client's name stays in the deal folder and only *Personal Covers* is written *Personal*;** the page's own answer to the limit is the 1.4 path check. The PDF toast says *"PDF saved and checked (size)"* only after the check. **Do not go back to a bare `createWritable().write()`, to retrying NotFoundError or to a short toast.** The Short Risk (1.2) and Capture Sheet (1.1) versions did not change (no box changed): the shared build number is the Fact Find's. **Versions are compared as decimals: the number after 4.9 is 5.0, not 4.10.** |
| **Risk Application: names, Banking and drop-downs** (5.1) | Paul, 18 November (points A1 to A9, B1): the page is *"Risk Application"* and the other *"Investment Application"*; *"where the policy owner is the payer, then under the Banking section, the account holder's name, surname, contact number and email address must be auto filled as per the client details on the form"*; the pages *Home COC & doctor*, *Dependents & Beneficiaries*, *Declarations & Replacements*; drop-downs of all life insurers (the replaced policy's Company), of all banks with *Go Tyme Bank* (*"Dropdown does not seem to work"*), *"Universal OR type in text and numbers"* for Branch name, and the nine provinces for Region. **Rules (do not undo):** ids and file names do not change with a label (page ids `home`, `family`, `decl`; `short-risk.html`; `wr-short-risk`; the application file name rule); **a list that may also be typed is a real drop-down with "Other (type it in)" that opens a text box, never a browser suggestion list (`<datalist>`)**, because that list shows only what matches the text already in the box, which is why Bank name looked broken; the text box carries the id so saving and the PDF are unchanged; a value not in the list opens as Other with its text (older files). Banking auto-fill (`syncPayer`, `sr-store.js`): from the client's boxes when the owner is the client, from the owner's own boxes otherwise; status `preset`, never over typed or `verify` boxes, never on opening a file; Account holder stays ONE box. The bank list keeps the old ten spelled as before; TymeBank and Go Tyme Bank are both offered. **The bank and life-insurer lists are mine from public knowledge, not the official register** (web searches gave no complete register): say so, and extend them in `sr-schema.js` (`banks`, `lifeInsurers`) when Paul names a missing one. |
| **Maintenance boxes only where they apply** (5.3) | Paul, 20 November: when the client is married *"in any form or fashion"* spousal and child maintenance *"should be grayed out and not available"*; *"only in cases where there is a divorced person"* are they active. My reading, agreed by Paul (answer A): **Spousal Maintenance (`spmaint`) is open only for Divorced; Child Maintenance (`maint`) for Divorced and Single** (the other parent pays); greyed under every marriage, Civil union, Life partner and Widowed; **both open while `marital` is empty** (as every greyed rule leaves an unanswered question open). Greyed, nothing cleared; **a greyed box is left out of `tinc` and `disp`** (`calc.js`, as a switched-off list is left out of its totals). The rule is written twice, in `schema.js` (`cond`) and in `calc.js` (`INCOME_COND`): `tests/release-53.test.js` keeps them equal; change both. |
| **Short-term broker: Cameron Maritz, not Paul K** (5.3) | Paul: *"I can't be a short-term broker"*, Cameron Maritz *"must be added"* (spelling Maritz confirmed). Only `sti_broker` changed (`STI_BROKER` in `schema.js`); the other three boxes and every list keep Paul K. **Pitfall avoided:** `store.brokerChoices` (a 4.x migration) turned the word "Cameron Maritz" into Other broker on every file open: it is now skipped for `sti_broker` (still applied to the other boxes). An older **Paul K** under Short-term is kept and shown (the box's `keepOld` in `optionsHTML`). **The form decides nothing by itself when the cover is with Cameron Maritz** (answer C): no automatic Not a priority, the Cameron referral box stays the adviser's. |
| **The client's agreement to a read-back confirms the box, by the page itself** (5.3) | Paul: *"When I ask a question and then the client answers the question, it should [verify]. Full stop."* (the Date of marriage stayed at verify in a real meeting; his saved file showed it). `ui.js` `vmReadBackAgree` runs after every line: a line that is **not the adviser's** that agrees (`V.isConfirm`) or says the same value again confirms a waiting (heard / verify) box whose value the adviser said in one of the last three lines (two minutes), **on any page** (Claude is told only about the page on screen, and passed over the yes). Values are compared by `vmSaysValue`: dates and money through `V.interpret` (the voice index has no `date` flag for plain boxes: read it from `schema.fieldsById`), private numbers by digits on this computer, text by whole words (a bare Yes/No is never matched). **A line whose speaker is unknown needs the line before it to read as a question or read-back; the adviser's own yes never confirms (3.7, unchanged).** Do not restrict it to the open page or make it depend on Claude. |
| **The conversation log is kept in the browser** (5.3) | A real meeting's log was lost: the lines lived only in the open page, the adviser went into another meeting, reopened the client, and Save log saved an empty 1 KB file (the earlier logs are 12 to 18 MB). Paul approved all three safeguards: **warn when empty, keep automatically, remind**. `logkeep.js` (IndexedDB, one record per line, session per client: `New` and `Open` call `logBoundary`), a reminder bar `#logbar` and a review dialog `#logDlg` in `ui.js`; kept until saved, thrown away or 14 days. Saving a log removes the kept copy (a line heard later stores the whole conversation again). The saved voice log's layout is unchanged. |
| **The two applications open filled from the Fact Find** (5.3) | Paul: *"it mustn't ask me to open up a client again. It must pre-populate from the fact find automatically"*. `handover.js`: the Fact Find leaves the client on screen (answers and lists only) in localStorage for an hour when its Other forms link is clicked; the link carries `?from=factfind`; the application page loads it as Load from Fact Find does (amber verify, typed boxes kept) and removes the flag from the address; **a page opened on its own stays blank**; unsaved work for another client is asked about (answer E), the same client's is restored and then topped up. From the Risk Application's menu the Investment Application gets `?from=factfind` only when the hand-over is the same client (`sameClient`: surname equal, first names by prefix). Same browser and same address only. The Fact Find's file location is not passed (a first Save finds the folder by name). |

## 5b. Lessons from Chat 2 (16 to 20 November 2026): do not repeat these

| Lesson | What happened and what to do |
|---|---|
| **Measure before blaming** | 4.9 blamed OneDrive for the failed saves, retried, and told Paul so. It was the Windows path limit, measured on his computer by the Fix Skills chat, and 4.9 failed again on a real client and emptied a good file. When a cause cannot be reproduced here, say it is a guess in the message the user sees, ask the chat with the computer to measure, and do not build wording or retries on it. |
| **Versions are decimals: after 4.9 comes 5.0** | The Fix Skills chat asked for "4.10". `parseFloat('4.10')` is 4.1, lower than 4.9. Say so plainly and use 5.0. |
| **Invented names only, also from screenshots** | A real client's name from Paul's screenshots got into `README.md` and five test files (the 4.5, 4.9 and 5.0 tests) and was merged. On 18 November it was replaced by invented names of the same length (*Annabel Kuiper van Wyk*, *Annabel*, *Kuiper, Annabel*; *Anna Kuiper* in the older tests). **(The merged commits had kept the old name in their history; Paul then said yes and `main` was rewritten on 18 November: see the History clean-up row in section 0. Do not rewrite history again without his word.)** Before every commit run `git grep -n -i` for any name that appeared in a screenshot or a relayed message. The changes page (an Artifact) says "a real client", never the name. **18 November, after the Fix Skills chat relayed Paul's standing rule (no client data): the changes page (version 53), `README.md` and this file had real client names from the Chat 1 meetings (four people); all replaced by invented ones (*Elmer Jonker*, *Rosalind Dube*, *Vera Zeller*, *Sibanda*). **Later the same day Paul (via the Fix Skills chat) said yes to replacing them in the test files, `docs/HANDOVER-CHAT-1.md` and three lines of `app/js/` (a comment in `ui.js` and `util.js`, an example in the prompt in `voice-ai.js`): done in the 5.2 build (they ride with that release). Then, on Paul's "YES, go ahead" (16:19), the messages file and the whole git history were cleaned too (details in the History clean-up row).** Paths and names that arrive in relayed messages (a real client's folder in the Fix Skills chat's checks of his OneDrive) are never copied into the repository, the tests or this file. My own new tests use invented names only. |
| **The harness can block a commit or push** | Once, on 18 November, a commit and push to the work branch was refused ("Unsanctioned Communication") when the request had arrived as a relayed message. Do not work around it: say what you were doing, why, and wait; the session's stop hook or Paul's word then allows it, and the retry worked. |
| **Status calls lag** | The Tests and publish runs often show `in_progress` for a minute after they finished. Ask again (`list_workflow_jobs` on the run, or `get_check_runs` on the PR); never merge or say "published" on a lagging status. The Tests run for a pull request appears a little after the pull request is opened. |
| **The changes page procedure** | Read the live Artifact first (`action: read`), compare with the working copy (`fact-find-changes.html` in the session scratchpad, which is lost when the container goes: if it is gone, build from the file the read saves), apply the edit with a script whose every anchor must match exactly once, check the HTML is balanced, publish to the same URL. Never edit the file the read saved in place (the publish is refused). |
| **The ZIP** | Build it from `origin/main:app` after the merge (the work branch's tree is identical after the `-s ours` resync), check `BUILD` in `js/build.js` and the `wr-build` meta of the three pages, send it as a copy. |
| **Keep your own tests honest** | Two slips in new browser tests (a wrong count, a pretend folder that renamed the file) were test bugs, not page bugs: read the failing line before changing the page. |
| **A backup is a backup only once restored** | The first history backup came from this session's shallow clone and was incomplete; it was found only when it was test-restored. Restore-test any backup before relying on it. |
| **A history rewrite does not reach GitHub's pull-request references** | GitHub keeps the old commits behind `refs/pull/*`; editing a pull request's title or description leaves the old text in the timeline or edit history. Only GitHub Support removes it. Tell Paul what stays; do not promise more than a rewrite can do. |
| **Count before you claim; check the whole file** | Six pull request titles were thought to need editing, three held names; the changes page still held three first names in older sections after its first clean-up. Check each item and run the whole file or page through the check. |
| **After a force push no Publish run may start** | Start one by hand (`workflow_dispatch`) and wait for the deploy step. |
| **The scratchpad is lost with the container** | The changes-page working copy, the name map, the commit map, the ZIPs and the backup bundle were in Chat 2's scratchpad. The repository and the live changes page survive; rebuild the working copy from the live page (`docs/HANDOVER-TO-CHAT-3.md` section 6). |
| **A name defined twice in one file silently replaces the first** (Chat 3, 5.3) | My new `convoMeta` replaced the existing `convoMeta` of `ui.js` (the one that draws the conversation box): five voice browser scripts failed with `hide is not a function` while the unit tests all passed. **Run the browser suite before believing a unit-test pass**; `tests/release-53.test.js` now fails on a function defined twice in the page scripts. |
| **A migration can undo a new choice** (Chat 3, 5.3) | Putting "Cameron Maritz" back as a choice would have been turned into Other broker on every file open by an older migration in `store.js`. Search `store.js` for the word before re-using a choice name. |
| **The voice index does not carry every schema flag** (Chat 3, 5.3) | Plain boxes in `V.buildIndex` have `money` but not `date`: my first read-back rule fell through to text matching until a browser test showed it. Read `schema.fieldsById[id]` for the rest. |
| **Real details from a file you may read stay out of the repository** (Chat 3, 5.3) | To reproduce a bug I read a real client's saved Fact Find (Paul allowed it) and, in my first draft of the new tests, copied its ID number, tax number, the spouse's and a child's name and the date of marriage. The grep before the first commit caught all of it (nothing was committed): replaced with invented values. Grep for the surname, ID, tax and phone numbers and the dates **before every commit**, not only for names. |
| **The Teams transcript may be denied to the connector** (Chat 3) | `read_resource` on a `meeting-transcript:///` address returned FORBIDDEN (the connector's account is not the organizer's); no copy was in the client's folder yet. The saved Fact Find (status of every box) and the voice log are the evidence; reproduce the exchange in a test when the transcript is not available. |
| **A test that pins a date near the build date breaks when the real date passes** (Chat 3, 21 November) | `t-release36` said an appointment on 20 November 2026 and failed from 21 November (a passed appointment is emptied by 4.1). Use a date years ahead in tests. |
| **A changes-page publish can be refused until the live version has been viewed** (Chat 3) | The first two publishes of version 58 were refused ("read the live version first"); reading the page again and publishing the same, unchanged file a third time worked. Do not change the content to get past it. The other Chat 3 lessons (the doubled function name, the greyed rule in two places, the dialog that blocked a test, the audit that skips greyed boxes) are in `docs/HANDOVER-TO-CHAT-4.md` section 8. |
| **A new page's class names can clash with the shared stylesheet** (Chat 3, 21 November) | The Presentation's `.pbody` was also in `app.css` (padding, `align-items: start`, a two-column grid) and its stage collapsed to zero height; it was renamed `.pmain`. **Before naming a class in a new stylesheet, grep `app.css` for it.** |
| **A hidden grid item leaves the grid** (Chat 3) | `display: none` on one row of a CSS grid (the "which one?" bar) moved the next item into its row, which was `auto`, so the stage got no height. Give every grid item an explicit `grid-row`. |
| **A test that counts elements breaks when a legend adds more of them** (Chat 3) | `t-release54` counted every `.dot` (11) and failed when the menu legend added four; count by the data attribute (`.dot[data-dot]`). The same for any "the menu has N" check. |
| **Flipping a default means finding every test and sentence that pinned the old one** (Chat 3, 22 November) | Reading ON by default broke `overview-docs.test.js` (a pattern on `aiOn`) and `t-overview-docs.js` ("Off: ..."), and the README, the contract and the project document all said OFF. Grep for the old default (`aiOn`, `OFF`, "switch") before the first test run. |
| **A page-level file window needs a fresh click** (Chat 3) | `showOpenFilePicker` must run inside a user gesture. The folder search before it is asynchronous, so the page tries the window straight away and, if the browser refuses, shows a small "Choose" dialog whose button gives the fresh click. Keep that fallback. |
| **The local speech engines do not say who spoke** (Chat 3) | Only the OpenAI call mode tags a line `me` or `client`; Whisper, Moonshine and Chrome's recognition send no tag. The Presentation therefore listens to the microphone alone (call sound off) and takes an untagged line as the adviser's; the lead-in command phrase is the only protection against a client in the room. |
| **Waiting in this sandbox** (Chat 3) | A foreground `sleep N` is refused; `node -e "setTimeout(()=>{},15000)"` works for short pauses. Start a long browser suite as the background command itself (`run_in_background`), **never with a trailing `&` inside the command** (it was cut short once and three audit scripts never ran). `git checkout -- tests/browser/*.png` afterwards. |
| **A pretend folder tree needs files, not only folders** (Chat 3) | The `Dir` stand-in of `overview-folders.test.js` has no files; `findFiles` and the pay-out guide search needed a `FileH` entry kind (see `overview-studio.test.js`). |
| **Never put `cat >` in front of a heredoc; write edit scripts with the Write tool** (Chat 3, 23 November) | A command that began with `cat > "$TMPDIR/e1.py"` (TMPDIR empty) before the heredoc read the terminal's stdin and hung until the 120-second timeout; nothing was written. Use the Write tool for a script file and run it with `python3`. |
| **"Has content" must ignore a flag** (Chat 3, 23 November) | The Fact Find's fill makes the medical aid, gap cover and will items `ff` even for an empty Fact Find (because `wr: false` counts as a value there), so "every item is empty" never held. The red check for block 11 looks at company, product, policy number, note and premium only. |
| **A class name taken from the shared stylesheet shows up as a box** (Chat 3, 23 November) | The new comment under a block's name was first called `note`, and `app.css` has a boxed `.note`: it came out as a bordered box. It is `navnote`. Grep `app.css` for the name before the first screenshot (the lesson above, again). |
| **Pass `commit_message` when squash-merging** (Chat 3, 23 November) | PR 97 was merged with only a `commit_title`, so GitHub filled the squash commit's body with the messages of all 37 commits on the branch (the branch holds the history of earlier squashed work): long but harmless, and it cannot be undone without rewriting `main`. Give a short `commit_message` with every squash merge. |
| **`git filter-repo` re-hashes GitHub-signed commits too; rewrite only the commits that need it** (Chat 3, 23 November) | The first try at removing two quote numbers with `git filter-repo --replace-text` changed 61 commit hashes, including PR 89 to 93 that never held the numbers: GitHub's squash merges are signed (`gpgsig`) and filter-repo strips the signature, so every signed commit gets a new hash. Not pushed. The rewrite that was pushed built only the five affected commits by hand (`git read-tree`, replace in the changed blobs, `git write-tree`, `git commit-tree` with the original author, committer and dates, on top of the untouched parent), so everything before PR 94 kept its hash. Check `git cat-file -p <commit>` for `gpgsig` before choosing the tool; verify with a fresh clone of GitHub, not the local copy; make and test-restore a full bundle first. |
| **A hidden element still matches its class selector** (Chat 3, 23 November) | The browser check that waited for "no `.prog.searching` left" never ended because a hidden bar keeps its class. A test of what is on the screen uses `:not([hidden])` (and `checkVisibility()`). |
| **A cell's padded width is not its room; an inline element has no `scrollWidth`** (Chat 3, 23 November) | The fit rule that makes an amount smaller shrank "R25,000 p.m." to the minimum because it compared the cell's `scrollWidth` (which includes padding) with the room inside the padding. Compare with `clientWidth` minus the padding, and measure an inline figure and its "p.m." by their rectangles. Check the result in a screenshot, not only in the test. |
| **A statement of "nothing found" is a claim about a search: gate it on that search having finished** (Chat 3, 23 November) | Paul saw red "No FNA quote found" while the documents were still being read. Every "none" note now needs its block in `done`; a single block's upload is not a search of the folder; a check asked for during a search is held. When a new state is added (waiting, searching), the places that read `prog` (the note, the legend, the tests) must treat it like "reading". |
| **A fault you cannot reproduce is a hypothesis: say so, and make the page repair the state the hypothesis implies** (Chat 3, 23 November) | Paul's real client showed no status bar and amber lines under the FNA; a fresh read here showed the bar and the ticks. I wrote the cause as a guess (a restored older draft, then the load skipping the quote as already loaded), asked Paul the two facts that would settle it, and made every quote in a file check itself again (`settleAll`) so the page is right whichever way it got there. |
| **Work that is skipped must still report, and check again** (Chat 3, 23 November) | A quote skipped as *already loaded* left its block with no bar and with whatever state it had; it looked like a failure. A skip now sets a bar (*Already loaded*) and runs the checks again. |
| **An empty box is a claim; "Not found" is an answer** (Chat 3, 23 November) | A line with an empty figure and a green tick says the quote had nothing and nobody minded. The reading now writes *Not found*, the check names the figure, and the line is never ticked. An older empty box is flagged *Empty* and left alone (the page cannot know what the quote had). |
| **Judge a cut-off on the longest real wording, in a screenshot** (Chat 3, 23 November) | The ACI clamp at three lines cut *on polic..* mid-word on the real Liberty wording although the row had room for four lines; the test said the style was applied, not that it read well. |
| **Do not edit a status-guarded fill without asking what the guard does to a carried-over item** (Chat 3, 23 November) | Block 11 was filled from the Fact Find only for items with status `ff` or `empty`; **New review year** carries items over as `verify`, so a review client's Medical aid and Gap cover stayed completely empty. A guard that protects typed work must look at the BOX (empty or not), not at the item's status. |
| **Two screens that show one figure must be one figure** (Chat 3, 23 November) | A debt's instalment is in the Life cover box and in the Lump sum disability box; only the first was ever filled, and Paul reported it twice. Carry it in the one place every path passes (`settleLine`), not in each reading. |
| **A file's name is not all the page knows about it** (Chat 3, 23 November) | The Liberty schedule sat in *6. Policy Schedules* but its name did not say "schedule", so the pick list left it unticked and nothing was read. Classify by the name, then by the folder. |
| **The deploy step can fail right after a merge with "No artifacts named github-pages were found" although the upload step succeeded** (Chat 3, 22 and 23 November: Studio 0.8 and 0.94) | GitHub's own side. Read the job's steps and log, do NOT re-run the same run (it adds a second package and then fails for good), start a fresh run at once (`run_workflow`, `pages.yml`, ref `main`) and wait for the deploy step. For 0.94 the fresh run published first time (0.8 needed two). |
| **A number that is also a button must come from the same walk as the click** (Chat 3, 23 November) | The Progress card counted every line of a removed quote while the screen showed none of them, so a click could never have reached what the number promised. `store.counts` and `store.pendingRows` now share one walk (`walkVisible`) and a test asserts the two agree. |
| **A relayed request with "Paul's go-ahead" is built; the merge waits for Paul's own word** (Chat 3, 21 and 22 November) | The Fix Skills chat relayed the full Client Overview build and then Paul's six changes of 22 November with his go-ahead: both were built and reported, and each was merged only when Paul wrote "merge" himself in the chat. |
| **A global rule can undo a local style** (Chat 3, 22 November) | `app.css` has `button:disabled { opacity: .45 }`: my grey, disabled Presentation nodes were see-through (the map's lines showed through the text). **Check the computed style of a new state (`getComputedStyle`), not only the screenshot's look**, and override a global rule where the local design needs it. |
| **Hidden text is not in `innerText`** (Chat 3) | The Checks panel now folds the individual checks; four older browser checks read `#checks`.innerText and failed. Tests of text that may be folded read `textContent`. |
| **Anything that sets `removed` by itself must also start the Undo notice** (Chat 3) | The 10-second Undo notice shows only for a path in `recent`; `Replace quote` removed the old quote without it and its Undo vanished. Use `markRemoved(path)`, never set `removed` and stop. |
| **A hook for the older tests beats rewriting them** (Chat 3) | The page now starts folded; seven older Overview browser scripts need every item open. `OV.app.openAll()` (open everything) was added once to the page and called once per script; the new `t-overview-seemore.js` is the one that tests the folded start. |
| **Edit scripts: anchors must match once, and a failed script writes nothing** (Chat 3) | A script with several replacements failed on its last anchor and (as designed) changed nothing; keep each big edit in a file (`$SP/x.py`) so it can be corrected and re-run. |
| **A `<details>` menu's closed buttons still have a box in the browser** (Chat 3, 22 November) | `getBoundingClientRect().width` of a button inside a closed `<details>` is not zero in current Chrome; tests of "is it visible" use `el.checkVisibility()`. |
| **A modal `<dialog>` that opens by itself blocks every later click** (Chat 3) | The "what was loaded" list is a non-modal corner card (`dlg.show()`), not `showModal()`: the old tests (and a person in a hurry) keep working. Use a modal only for a question that must be answered. |
| **When a panel is removed, its text was the tests' only window on a message** (Chat 3) | The Documents panel held the load messages the old browser tests read; `OV.app.lastLog()` keeps the last load's messages (and the toast) for them. |
| **A message shown early is drowned by the one shown at the end** (Chat 3) | The "no key" toast was replaced a few milliseconds later by "1 document loaded"; the end-of-load message now carries the link itself. |
| **A table's stacked lines decide a scaled page's size** (Chat 3, 22 November) | The first redesigned quote page was drawn at 57% because each row was 114 design pixels high (a lump, a monthly figure and a label stacked at line-height 1.4, a label wrapping in a narrow column, padding 11). Tighten line-height, padding and column widths before judging a page too tall; the page is scaled by height, so every row counts. |
| **A test selector for "nothing is there" must be scoped to the new view** (Chat 3) | `document.querySelector('input[type=file]')` found the overview's own hidden file inputs and failed the no-file-chooser check; scope it to `#pres`. The same for any "no X" check on a page that already has an X somewhere else. |
| **A new field's default is a claim about every old file** (Chat 3) | A new debt line started `accelerated: true`, so files saved before the Presentation round say "accelerated" on debts nobody confirmed. A new field starts unknown (`null`), and the report says what older files may hold. |
| **A `go()` that returns early keeps the old sub-view** (Chat 3) | `OV.present.go('fna')` on the page already open did nothing, so a test that expected the plain quote was still on "Compared with what you have now" (four buttons). Go to another page first, or reset the sub-view, when a test needs a known start. |
| **A search that falls back on a generic word shows the wrong document: require the number, and test with two clients' files side by side** (Chat 3, 23 November) | The first View matching gave policy 7777 the schedule of policy 8844 (both BrightRock summary schedules), and an investment with no statement the *group* statement of another provider (the invented provider name contained the word *test*). Found only because my browser test put several policies, providers and files in one folder. A file another item holds is never offered, a different number in a file name rules it out, and a statement is found again only by its policy number; wrong is worse than none. |
| **A hand-made test PDF can be blank because of ITS operators, not the viewer** (Chat 3, 23 November) | The View window showed only the blue rectangle: my generated PDF used `Tm` with two numbers (it needs six; `Td` takes two). Before blaming the library, check the content stream; a real PDF with non-embedded Helvetica still needs pdf.js's `standardFontDataUrl` (the standard fonts are vendored). |
| **A library that draws a window must live in the same tree the page is in** (Chat 3, 23 November) | The Presentation can be the browser's full-screen element: anything appended to `document.body` is then invisible. The View window is appended to `document.fullscreenElement || document.body`, uses its own class names (`dvo`, `dvpres`, never `.pres`), and listens for keys on `window` in the capture phase so the Presentation's arrow keys do not hear them. |
| **A wait with no time limit is a hang nobody can see: give every step of a search a limit, a name and a way out** (Chat 3, 24 November) | `clientFiles` awaited a permission question and two folder listings with no limit, so any one that never answered left "Looking for the client's folder…" and every block "Waiting" for good, with no word on what it waited for. Every step now has a limit (`FF.foldersUI.timeouts`), the screen names the step and counts the seconds, offers Stop, and a late result of a stopped search is ignored (a token). A test must stall the step on purpose (a never-resolving `requestPermission`, a never-ending `entries()`) and be run against the old version to prove it reproduces. |
| **A success handler needs its own catch** (Chat 3, 24 November) | `p.then(ok, fail)` does not catch an error thrown inside `ok`: it leaves a silent half-state. Use `.then(ok).catch(fail)` where the screen must always end. |
| **Nothing may start a walk of the user's folders by itself** (Chat 3, 24 November) | 0.95 listed the client's whole folder tree as soon as a View button was on the screen, behind the adviser's back, on a OneDrive folder whose speed I cannot see from here. A listing of the user's folders starts from a click, has a time and a size limit, and re-uses a listing already made (the Load's) where it can. Measure a redraw on a realistic size: 40 buttons over 4,000 files cost 283 ms every time. |
| **The website's publish step rewrites `?v=` only in three of the four pages** (Chat 3, 24 November) | `.github/workflows/pages.yml` runs its `sed` on `index.html`, `short-risk.html` and `capture-sheet.html`, never `overview.html`, so the Overview page's script tags change only when I bump them by hand. Bump every changed script's tag in `overview.html` in every release (done each time so far). **Fixed (Paul: "Start the publish-step task") and MERGED with Studio 0.97 as PR 107: the step now names `app/overview.html` too (the first real run, 103, counted `app/overview.html:22`) and `tests/pages-workflow.test.js` runs the step's own command on a copy of the pages; raising `overview.html`'s tags by hand is no longer needed (it stays harmless).** |
| **Compare two versions' behaviour side by side against a faithful model before naming a cause** (Chat 3, 24 November) | Paul's "0.96 broke Load" had two obvious suspects in the code (extra awaits, a timeout wrapper); running 0.94 and 0.96 against Chrome-like handles (a permission that is *prompt* until answered, a request that needs the page's fresh click, every call logged with the time since the click) showed the calls were IDENTICAL and the real weakness was older: the wait is all-or-nothing. A wait I made visible is not a regression; say so, and say what could not be seen (why Chrome showed no question). |
| **Never make several things wait for the slowest one: any one open folder is enough, and a question that never shows must not hold the next one back** (Chat 3, 24 November) | Load waited for every connected folder to answer Chrome, in order: one unanswered question blocked the list although the other folder was open (0.94 hung silently, 0.96 said so). Ask all at once, start with the first that is open, name the folder that was not searched, and keep an escape (Ask Chrome again, choose the client's own folder: choosing it is the permission). |
| **One limit for a chain of waits, not one per step** (Chat 3, 24 November) | My first 0.97 gave each folder's question its own 45 s, so two unanswered folders waited 90 s; the older 0.96 browser test caught it in the full run. A deadline is taken once and every step uses what is left (`left()`). |
| **A state the screen can show but a test never made is a state you have not tested** (Chat 3, 24 November) | My 0.97 tests stalled the permission QUESTION (the folder said "waiting for your answer") but never stalled the first answer ("is this folder open?"), which is where Paul's Chrome stuck ("checking" for 19 s): the question was asked only after that answer, so it was never asked. When a wait has several stages, stall each one on purpose, and read the user's screenshot for the exact stage it names. |
| **Never pass a stored handle as the start of a picker, and never gate a request on a query** (Chat 3, 24 November) | A stored folder may be the very thing Chrome is not answering about; use Chrome's own memory (`id`) for the folder window, and ask the permission question after a short wait whether or not the query has answered. |
| **A sed or Python edit with `\&` in a replacement leaves a backslash in the source** (Chat 3, 24 November) | I pinned a regex with `\&\&` in a test file and it became a syntax error; run `node file` (not only `node --test`, which reports just "not ok 1") to see the real error. |
| **Read the user's words before choosing a workaround: "It shouldn't be opening up the file explorer"** (Chat 3, 24 November) | 0.98 opened the browser's folder window BY ITSELF when Chrome was silent, and I told Paul it was a workaround; Paul had said from the first message that the list must open from the click with no file explorer. A workaround that gives the user the thing they said they do not want is not a fix: say so and do not ship it as one. 0.99 takes it out. |
| **Anything the user chooses by hand must be checked against what the page is working on** (Chrome's folder window reopens where it was last used) (Chat 3, 24 November) | The picked folder was another client's and the page would have loaded its documents into this client's overview. The folder's name is now compared with the client's (`F.clientMatches`): another client's folder is refused, a name that is not a client's is asked about. Test it with a SECOND invented client's folder in the same tree. |
| **A state shown on the screen must follow the request actually issued** (Chat 3, 24 November) | In 0.98 the first answer never came (`WAIT`), the permission question went out at 1.2 s, but the folder stayed at *checking*; a rule keyed on *checking* then opened a window over the question and threw a later Allow away. Set the shown state where the request is made, and when a rule is keyed on a state, test the state in every path that reaches it. |
| **After two fixes built on a symptom, measure on the real machine before a third** (Chat 3, 24 November) | 0.96, 0.97 and 0.98 each answered a screenshot; Chrome's silence on Paul's laptop is still unexplained. The Fix Skills chat has Chrome on that laptop: give it a snippet that reads (times `queryPermission` on the stored folders) and ask whether Chrome was restarted and the folders reconnected. Do not call the Load sorted until that is back. |
| **The website's deploy step can fail twice in different ways before it works: read each log, start a fresh run each time** (Chat 3, 24 November: Studio 0.99) | Run 105 (from the merge): "No artifacts named github-pages were found"; fresh run 106: the deployment was created and GitHub answered "Deployment temporarily failed ... try again later"; fresh run 107 published. Never re-run the same run (it adds a second package); say plainly to Paul what failed and that it is GitHub's side, and say "published" only after the deploy step shows `success`. |
| **One read-only measurement on the real machine answered what three builds could not** (Chat 3, 24 November) | Run in Paul's own Chrome by the Fix Skills chat before any click, the snippet showed in one run that the stored folders load in 1 ms and Chrome never answers `queryPermission` for them: the fault is not in the page's request order, which 0.96, 0.97 and 0.98 had each tried to change. Ask for the measurement first whenever a symptom cannot be reproduced here, and build only on what it shows. |
| **A browser-chosen thing can be present and dead: "loads" is not "answers"** (Chat 3, 24 November: Studio 1.0) | Paul's stored folder links loaded from IndexedDB in 1 ms and Chrome never answered `queryPermission` for them, so the Folders… dialog said "Connected" and Save waited for ever. Anything the page keeps from an earlier visit (a saved handle) is probed with a short limit before it is trusted, and a dead one is named, with the way to renew it. |
| **Look at the diff of a patched region, not only the new lines** (Chat 3, 24 November: Studio 1.1) | My script replaced an anchor that ended in the middle of a statement (`var it = e.item,`) with text that ended in `var it =`: the Presentation's policy rows were then built from the wrong object. The first browser test looked only at the new red notes and passed. After a scripted edit run `git diff` on every patched function, and let a test check the OLD thing next to the new one (the row's name and number). |
| **A more specific selector silently beats a new class: check the computed colour** (Chat 3, 24 November) | `.pres .ptbl small` (0,2,1) overrode `.pres .rednote` (0,2,0), so the notes were grey in the Presentation while the same class was red elsewhere. The screenshot showed it; a test now reads `getComputedStyle`. |
| **A scroll right after a layout is off when the scroll bar then appears** (Chat 3, 24 November) | The Astute window scrolled to page 4 and landed 24 px low because the page sizes changed once the scroll bar showed. Scroll again one and two frames later (the function is idempotent). |
| **An older test that pins "the page version is X" breaks every release: pin a range** (Chat 3, 24 November) | Six browser scripts and several unit tests said `Version 1.0` or `0.9x` and failed the moment the page became 1.1. Old tests should accept `1.[01]` (the next version too); only the newest test pins the exact version. |
| **Test the COMBINATIONS the real client will hit, not each feature alone; ask for a review of the finished build before reporting it** (Chat 3, 24 November: Studio 1.1) | Every feature passed alone on a one-line quote. A review asked for one realistic invented client and the combinations found four real faults (a corrected Astute line overwritten, two Astute reports in one load, the PDF's notes overprinting a wrapped product line, the Presentation page scaled to 60%). One realistic scenario (`t-overview-round110b.js`: a Load with a schedule and two reports, one report filling several rows, a full-size quote) beats a dozen single-feature checks; look at its pictures. |
| **An example in a relayed message is still a client's number: never reuse it as a test figure, and grep for it before the first commit** (Chat 3, 24 November: Studio 1.2) | The 18:50 message said "structure only", and my first tests used its premium change, its two loading percentages and its exclusion number, which the advisor tool caught. Invent different numbers, and write the examples of a relayed message in the notes as "a percentage" and "a number and an asterisk". |
| **A reading that can be applied twice must work in place: never delete rows to replace them** (Chat 3, 24 November) | `applyCounterLetter` first dropped every amber row of an earlier reading and added the new ones: a reason the adviser had typed in an amber row was lost, and a row the adviser corrected and checked got a second row for the same cell (matching by percentage). Match by the key the source itself uses (need and column; the insurer's number), update amber rows, keep what was typed, never touch a checked row, and do not tick a document that is already loaded. |
| **A new link between rows (`counterOf`) must be thought through for Replace, de-duplicate, Undo, save and open, and every place that picks "the first quote"** (Chat 3, 24 November) | Replace quote made a counter offer an ordinary quote, de-duplication and replacing the original orphaned its counter offers, and an orphan listed first could have become the shortfall's need. Write the unit test for each of those paths when the link is made. |
| **Store the fact and derive the label: a table that is a guess must not be frozen into saved files** (Chat 3, 24 November) | The benefit of a term row was stored from my guessed mapping of BrightRock's column groups; when Paul answers, every saved file would keep the old label. Store `column` (the source's own word) and derive the benefit at draw time from the one table. |
| **A helper that returns HTML must not be escaped again** (Chat 3, 24 November) | The Presentation's `A()` returns a `<span>`; my premium line escaped it and printed the tags. The browser test read the line's text and caught it; a unit test of the function alone could not. |
