| **A reviewer found the same unlimited wait in Save that I had fixed in Load** (Chat 3, 24 November) | After fixing every wait in Load I did not grep the other callers of the same permission helper: `F.permission(handle, true)` in Save's `open()` had no limit since 0.94. When a cause is found in one path, grep every other use of the same call before saying the cause is handled. |
| **A locator by text can click the wrong element** (Chat 3, 24 November) | `text=Reconnect my folders` matched the bold words in the paragraph before the button, the click did nothing and the test timed out; use `button:has-text("...")`. The picker stand-in's `__pickNext` also answered the Folders… dialog's own pickers and made both folders "View, Vera": a stand-in that answers every call with one thing hides which call it was. |
| **A number that is also a count of things on the screen must be tested against the screen** (Chat 3, 24 November: Fact Find 5.5) | The Progress card said 30 to verify while every pill was green: it counted notes boxes that never drew a pill and boxes a rule hides or greys out. The 4.2 test only checked the list against itself. The browser test now sets amber answers on several pages (one greyed, one not drawn) and asserts the card equals the amber pills the adviser can act on, summed over every page, and that every click of the number lands on one; run against the old code it counts 7 for 3 pills. |
| **Look at the published page's own text for placeholders** (Chat 3, 24 November: changes page 68) | Version 67 printed ' + TICK + ' in the status cells of two sections, eight times, because a script wrote a placeholder that nothing replaced; neither the balance check nor my reading of the build script caught it. Before publishing the changes page, render the file in Chromium, read its text for stray quotes, plus signs and variable names, and check the tiles and the new headings. |
| **Reproduce the user's symptom with an invented file before naming the cause** (Chat 3, 24 November) | I read the two counting functions, formed the theory (a notes box counted without a pill, hidden boxes counted) and proved it in 20 lines of Playwright with an invented client before saying so; the first run of my own test then showed a THIRD case (a greyed box is drawn, grey, with its amber pill) that the theory had missed. Say what was reproduced and what was not, and give the person with the real file a read-only snippet that classifies each amber answer. |
| **A rule can hide a box in two ways: not drawn, or drawn grey and inert** (Chat 3, 24 November) | `f.cond` false gives nothing when the box has no `grey` flag and a greyed (`off`, opacity .45, inert) box when it has one; `adding a debt row says the client has debts` (`has_debts` flips to Yes), so a test that sets No must do it AFTER adding the row; text typed into a box is stored on `change` (blur), not on each key. |
| **A finding from another tool's text extraction is not a fault of the page until the page is seen to have it** (Chat 3, 24 November: Studio 1.3) | The skill saw BrightRock's private *ti* glyphs and the exclusion number inside the wording in its text extraction; the page sends the PDF itself to Claude. I first wrote that all five findings were real faults; the advisor tool corrected me. Build the safety net, say it is one, and ask the person with the real file to run the page's own flow and report the structure. |
| **An example in a relayed message can end up in a PROMPT, not only in a test: grep the prompts and the scripts too** (Chat 3, 24 November) | Two of my reading prompts carried a real exclusion number as their example and had been on `main` since 1.2; the new unit test reads the page scripts for it. |
| **When two documents print the same figure a cent apart, pick the source, align it where it is read and test both orders of reading** (Chat 3, 24 November) | The letter's revised premium and the counter offer quote's premium differ by rounding; choosing the letter inside the one `counterPremium` function would have left the quote's bar, the issued policy and the policy row printing the other figure. The quote's premium is made the letter's (within 5 cents) when the second document is read, whichever comes first; a real difference is left and checked. |
| **A test that mimics a typed correction by assigning a field is not a typed correction** (Chat 3, 24 November) | The old test set `what` directly; the page marks a typed row `by: person` only through `setPath`, so the new clean-up rightly refreshed it. Use the same path the adviser's typing takes. |
| **Do not build a more elaborate version of a guess while the question is open: wait for the answer, or build the simplest reading** (Chat 3, 24 November) | I refined my own guess about BrightRock's column groups (the need group deciding between severe illness and income protector under the same column) twice while Paul's answer was on its way; his answer was simpler (four separate pillars, never a combined label) and the refinement was thrown away. One round of tests and records was spent on it. |
| **A correction can arrive minutes after the message it corrects: when two messages disagree, build the earlier, settled one and wait for the dust before building the later in full** (Chat 3, 24 and 25 November) | Paul's 22:27 message (four pillars, one per pillar) and the 22:30 clarification (references on every need line with an amount) came three minutes apart; I built the second in full, with a browser test that asserted it cell by cell, and the next morning's correction (per pillar, not per line) removed it. The 22:30 text said where references go on a line, which the 22:27 text did not ask for. |
| **A warning that names two figures must keep both: never overwrite one silently and promise to warn about it** (Chat 3, 25 November) | The 1.3 premium rule overwrote the counter offer quote's premium with the letter's when the two were within 5 cents, so the warning Paul asked for ("even by 1 cent") could never fire and the proposal's own figure was gone from the file. The advisor tool caught it before the build: the proposal's figure is kept (`offer.quotePremium`), recorded when the quote is read and, for a file that has none, when it opens; the warning compares the two in cents. |
| **A fold inside a card loses its state on the redraw that typing causes** (Chat 3, 25 November) | Typing in a field inside *Correct by hand* redraws the page and closes the `<details>`: its state is kept in a map keyed by the card (`keepOpen`, a `toggle` listener in the capture phase because the event does not bubble), and the older browser tests' `openAll()` opens it too. The same for the Presentation: `render()` rebuilds the slide, so an exclusion's folded state lives in `ps.exShut`. |
| **A name that identifies a file must include its folder as soon as folders hold many files of the same name** (Chat 3, 25 November) | `uniqueFiles`, the `dirs` table of the load and the View button's `src.file` all knew a file by its NAME; one policy folder's `Policy Schedule.pdf` would have dropped the next, and every policy would have opened the first. A File now carries `wrDir`, `wrGroup`, `wrBlock`, de-duplication adds the folder for files of a policy folder, and the View picker chooses by folder (policy number, then insurer); a test puts two same-named files in two folders and one in two years. Do this check whenever a listing starts to include more folders. |
| **One folder that never answers must cost its own slice, not the rest: name everything first, then walk with a slice each** (Chat 3, 25 November) | The first walk went through the policy folders one after the other under one limit: one hung folder left every later folder unnamed and unread, and the standard-folder listing (which also entered every policy folder for its Comparative Quotes) hung for the whole 60-second limit. Every live folder is named before any is walked, each walk has its own slice (`TIMEOUTS.policyFolder`), the three searches run together, and the list says which folders were not (fully) read. The test hangs one folder's `entries()` on purpose. |
| **A "not found" chip must not be shown for a folder that was not finished** (Chat 3, 25 November) | The first partial list said *No schedule or statement found* for the folder that never answered, which is a claim about the folder; it now says *Not (fully) read*. The same rule as the "nothing found" lesson of 0.91: a verdict about a search needs the search to have finished. |
| **A tie is said, not guessed; but the same file kept in two places is not a tie** (Chat 3, 25 November) | "Newest" is told by the date in the name, else the year folder. Two different files on the same date tick none and the list says so; the same file name in the policy folder and in `6. Policy Schedules` is one document (the policy folder's copy is taken). My first test expected a summary to beat a schedule on the same year and both words count the same: the test, not the rule, was wrong. |
| **Tables of limits are pinned by older tests: widen the pin, not the code** (Chat 3, 25 November) | Adding `policyFolder` to `TIMEOUTS` failed three older source-pattern tests that spelled the whole table out; each now allows the new entry. |
| **When the real document's structure arrives after the build, check the build against it before asking for more: the gate and the reader may already fit, or a local reader may beat a model call** (Chat 3, 25 November) | The Fix Skills chat sent the structure of a real underwriter's reply (the subject has both Cover Proposal numbers; one reason paragraph with a fixed lead-in and end). My number gate already fitted it; the general reading (a Claude call, which sends a client's medical reason out) could be replaced for the exact wording by a plain text reader on the adviser's computer, with the model call kept only as a fallback. Ask Paul first (he asked for options, one question at a time), then build. |
| **When a design removes a structure, older browser scripts that select by that structure fail: read each failing line before changing it** (Chat 3, 25 November) | The two older 1.2 and 1.3 scripts read table rows and amber lines that had moved into the new block; each failure was the test pinning the old layout, not the page, and each was rewritten to say what is now true (the PDF's exclusion row has no type column; the premium warning legitimately names the figure the proposal printed). |
| **A rule keyed on a date cannot rule an item that has no date: it must follow the item it belongs to** (Chat 3, 25 November: Studio 1.6) | I put the pay-out guides in the same year rule as the quotes, but a guide's name has no date: a current guide loose in the quotes folder lost to an old guide in a year folder and neither was ticked. A guide follows its quote's CP number. The advisor review found it because my own fixture had moved both guides loose; put the awkward layout in the fixture. |
| **A document that is a quote only by the folder it sits in must not be ticked: read as a quote it leaves an empty card** (Chat 3, 25 November) | An *RA Review* document in a review folder was classed as a comparative quote by its folder, ticked and read, and the page made an empty quote card from a document that is not a quote. The browser test now looks at the block's state after Load, not only at what was sent. Listed, labelled, unticked. |
| **The saved file holds a file's name only: when two files share one name, choose the newest and say so** (Chat 3, 25 November) | The View button could open the 2024 *FNA Quote.pdf* when the 2026 one had been loaded. No key may be added to the saved file for a display change, so `sameName` narrows by policy number or insurer and then by the newest year. |
| **Never replace a count or a number in a record file with a blanket find and replace: it rewrites the history rows too** (Chat 3, 25 November) | My first records script replaced every `1,148` and `2,195` in CLAUDE.md, which also changed the counts that belong to the 1.5 row and its history line; the diff showed it and I redid the edit with exact anchors. Read `git diff` of a record file before committing it. |
| **When a rule is buried in a screen function, move it out before changing it: a pure function can be tested; a screen function can only be looked at** (Chat 3, 25 November: Studio 1.7) | The year rule of 1.6 lived inside `tickPlan` (which needs the whole page); Paul's change to it (the date rule) was made as `FF.folders.ageQuotes`, a pure function with 14 unit tests (each kind on its own, the same date, no date at all, a month with no day, the guides), and the browser test now only pins what the list shows. |
| **When the approved design is the spec, compare the build with it box by box and list every deviation before reporting** (Chat 3, 25 November: Studio 2.0) | My tests checked my own design. The advisor review made me re-read the eight saved boards and found what I had left out: the *Type of servicing* box, the menu counts, *Not requested*, the note under the lines, and several differences I had not named (now → new inside a cell, no running-order slide, one PDF page for each block). Re-read the spec text itself (the boards are saved as `.dc.html` files) before the report, and name each deviation with its reason. |
| **A fallback that guesses a link is worse than no link** (Chat 3, 25 November: Studio 2.0) | I tied a servicing quote to "the only policy of that insurer" when the folder's number matched nothing: a quote of ANOTHER policy would have been drawn against the wrong schedule. Tie by the identifying number only, keep the number when the target is not there yet (found when it is), say so when no policy matches, and test with a second policy of the same insurer whose figures differ a little. |
| **A test fixture that flips because of a new rule may be showing a behaviour change, not a stale pin** (Chat 3, 25 November) | The older folder test's FNA quote (a deal-style folder with no policy number) became a servicing FNA with no policy. Ask what the rule should do with that real shape before editing the test: the rule was narrowed (a servicing folder names its policy) and the old test restored. |
| **Check every place that lists the blocks by hand when a block is added** (Chat 3, 25 November) | `grep shortfallQuote` found the View roles (`ov-view.js`) and the document chooser (`blockChoices`) that I had missed; the check pass also left a *Searching* bar on the empty servicing blocks. Search the page scripts for an existing block id, not only for the schema's own list. |
| **Never write the example of a relayed message into a fixture, even its date or its circumstance** (Chat 3, 25 November: Studio 2.0, again) | My fixture names reused the date and the *new bond* of the real example; the advisor review caught it before the push. Invent a different date, a different circumstance and different numbers, then `git grep` the relayed values before every push. |
| **A name that carries the client's SURNAME makes every substring test unsafe: cut the client out of the name first** (Chat 3, 25 November: Studio 2.1) | The new naming guide ends every quote's name with `(<Initials Surname>)`; `classify` tests the name for *rgar*, *egar*, *shortfall*, *letter*, *owner* and the like, so `(A Vergara)`, `(A Margaritis)` and `(Z Egarton)` made a BR FNA a goal report and `(K Shortfalls)` a shortfall quote. Found only because the advisor tool asked for surnames and premiums in the fixtures: my fixtures used `(S Test)` and `(A Tester)`. Put awkward surnames (and premiums such as `R2026.00`, which looks like a year) into every fixture of a naming rule. |
| **A rule keyed on the year folder cannot tell two files of one year apart: say which rule breaks the tie, and test it with two files in ONE folder** (Chat 3, 25 November) | Paul's "newest only" held across year folders (1.6) and for a kind with no year folder (1.7), but two servicing quotes in `2027 - Review` were both ticked: the date rule ran only when no year folder existed. Now the five servicing kinds break the tie by the date in the name inside the newest year; new business quotes keep the earlier rule. |
| **A fixture that returns a made-up insurer hides the real case** (Chat 3, 25 November) | The stand-in reading answered *Other Co* for a BrightRock `BR Quote`, so the comparative card, the PDF page and the missing-guide check were never looked at for the real shape. Make the stand-in answer what the real document would. |
| **When a page rule changes, give the old fixtures what the new rule needs instead of loosening their tests** (Chat 3, 25 November) | Four unit tests and four browser scripts counted twelve pages; the PDF fixtures now carry a shortfall and a comparative quote (a quote page prints only with a quote) and keep their twelve pages; the tests that pinned an empty overview say what is now true (the five pages of Where the client is now). |
| **I put `cat >` in front of a heredoc again** (Chat 3, 25 November) | The second time (the lesson is on this page since 23 November): a 120 s hang, nothing written, a background task to stop. Use the Write tool for every script file, always, and run it with `python3`. |
| **A key on the container is lost when the loader rebuilds the container from its parts: test Save and Open for every new key** (Chat 3, 25 November: Studio 2.2) | `fromJSON` rebuilt every block from its `quotes` or `items` only, so the adviser's `blocks.<id>.needed` would have been saved and then dropped on Open. Found by my own unit test of `fromJSON(toJSON(state))`. A key added anywhere on the saved file needs that round trip in its test. |
| **When a decision is derived from two sources, the second must not read a placeholder of the first as a result** (Chat 3, 25 November) | The Fact Find's own proposed-investment row counted as *loaded*, so a proposal that was asked for could never be outstanding; a count of "things there" must say which things (filled by a document or the adviser, not the request itself). The advisor tool asked what the sample showed: `proposed:loaded` straight after the fill. |
| **A value that the page decides by a name must be decided again after what happens to it** (Chat 3, 25 November) | The auto-tick used the block `classify` named; a bare Serv quote lands in increase or reduction by its premium (the line below already read the landing block). Use the place a thing ended up, not the place it was filed under, and test the case where they differ. |
| **A last year's request is not this year's: a copy for a new period must drop what was derived from the old period's source** (Chat 3, 25 November) | New review year cloned the whole `factFind`, `asked.blocks` with it: the new year's lines would have started as asked for. Drop what the new year's Fact Find has to say again, and test the new-year path for every new derived key. |
| **A rule that can leave a screen with nothing ticked and nothing said must say so** (Chat 3, 25 November) | A Fact Find that asks for no quote made every quote go into a collapsed group; the dialog now opens the group and says why. Run a scenario in which the rule's input is empty, not only the one where it picks something. |

## 6. Not yet tested with a real client

**Paul's API key** (Console > API keys; the form's Settings holds the key and its expiry date, per browser and per copy): keys last 30 days and are renewed by hand; the top bar counts the days down (amber at 14). The key is renewed by Paul in the console; never ask him to paste a key into the chat.

The automated tests use invented answers and a faked Claude. Not yet proved on a real client: **the Client Overview (all of it, 0.1 to 0.5: never seen on Paul's screen or with a real document; the Presentation's voice never heard a real microphone, a real speech model or a projector)**; **Fact Find 5.3 / Risk Application 1.5 / Investment Application 1.4** (built and merged on 20 November, not yet used by Paul: the Date completed Calendar, the greyed maintenance boxes, the short-term broker Cameron Maritz, the page's own confirmation of a read-back, the kept conversation log with its reminders, the Address, COC & doctor page, the Other forms menus and the applications opening filled from the Fact Find: tested on the real pages in my own screenshots, not on Paul's screen; **whether the client's voice reaches the form on a Teams call, which the read-back rule depends on, is still the open Teams-call item below**); **listening to a real Teams call** (PR 10: share the call's sound, needs a short test call); **voice for "work cell"**; **"What was covered"** (pages, not topics); **the passed-appointment question**, the **Progress buttons and Verify All**; the **action-point sources rule** with the real Claude (if it ignores the format, all its points are removed and the plain points stay, and the Admin list says so); reading **scanned policy schedules** (the skills can, this sandbox cannot); **4.4 and 4.5 on Paul's own screen** (in the one-line meetings *Teams Meeting* and long names may be cut off in the box: ask Paul; 4.6 has been checked by Paul on his screen and works); **Capture Sheet 1.1** (the new file names, in the browser's Save window with a real folder) is seen only in my test screenshots and with a pretend Save window; **Fact Find 5.2 (Save into the client's `2. Fact Find` folder)** (merged, published and installed, but seen only on a pretend folder tree and on a real directory tree in the browser's private file system, in my own screenshots; **not on Paul's OneDrive**); **Risk Application 1.4 / Investment Application 1.3 (Save into the client's Applications folder)** (merged, published and installed on Paul's computer at 15:25, but not yet seen working by Paul: seen only on a pretend folder tree and on a real directory tree in the browser's private file system, in my own screenshots; **not on Paul's OneDrive, not with Chrome's own folder picker and its permission prompts, and the real IndexedDB round trip of a connected folder's handle is untried because headless Chromium here dies when it reads such a handle back**; the alias table is mine but was checked by the Fix Skills chat against what the skill writes); **5.1** (merged, published and installed on Paul's computer at 14:20: the new names, Banking auto-fill and the drop-downs: tested on the real page in my own screenshots and scripts, not on Paul's screen; the bank and life-insurer lists are mine, not the official register; **Paul has not yet seen it**); **5.0** (the path-limit message, no retry for NotFoundError, a failed Save counted as unsaved: tested with a pretend folder whose close() throws NotFoundError and empties the file, on all three pages; **the cause itself was measured on Paul's computer, but 5.0 has not yet been seen on his screen**: ask Paul or the Fix Skills chat to save a Short Risk file once with the shorter deal folder, and to tell us if the new message appears; **whether the shorter deal folder fixes every case is the skill's change, not tested here**); **4.9** (the verified write on a real folder: it worked for the files that fit, and its message was wrong for the path limit); **4.8** (the bold headings in the two boxes, and the read view that a click opens for typing in the Priorities box and the Action Points) is installed on Paul's computer, but I have seen it only in my test screenshots and Paul has not yet used it on a real client; **4.7** (the High blood sugar label, and what real Claude does with a spoken "diabetes", now that the page no longer picks the box for that word) is installed on Paul's computer, but I have seen it only in my test screenshots and whether Paul has looked at it is not recorded. The full list of open items is in `docs/HANDOVER-CHAT-1.md` section 6. I cannot open the live website or Paul's OneDrive reliably from this sandbox.

---

**The rules below were set earlier by Paul and stay exactly as written.**

## Keep README.md up to date (set by Paul Kafentzis, always)

- **Every change that a user can see or that changes how the app, the tests or the publishing work is written into `README.md` in the same commit as the change.** That means a new, moved, renamed or removed page, box, label, choice, list, check, button or setting; a changed rule or calculation; a changed file name; and a change to how the tests or the website publishing run.
- A statement the change makes untrue is **corrected or deleted**, not left beside the new one. Search the README for the old wording before finishing (page order, labels, ids, counts, file paths).
- A change is not finished, and is not offered for merging, until `npm test` passes, which includes `tests/readme.test.js` (the README's page order and the file paths in its Layout table are checked against the code).

## Keep the Fact Find PDF mirroring the form (set by Paul Kafentzis, always)

- **The PDF always shows the latest form, exactly.** Every change to what the form shows (a new, moved, renamed or removed page, heading, box, label, note, choice, default, list column, quote table or summary line, or a changed wording) is made in the PDF in the same commit as the change. The PDF is built from the form's own definition (`app/js/schema.js`) by `app/js/pdf.js`, so a box in the schema appears by itself; the parts `pdf.js` draws by hand (the priorities and quotes page, the BrightRock Risk Calculation page, the quote tables, the lists and the Admin page) must be updated by hand.
- **A change is not finished, and is not offered for merging, until the PDF has been checked against the form**: `npm test` passes, which includes `tests/pdf-mirror.test.js` (every box, heading and note of the form is in the PDF, under the same words), and so does the browser check `tests/browser/audit-pdf.js` (the text of every screen against the text of a filled PDF; run with `npm run test:browser`). A new thing on the form that neither check can see (it is not a schema box and not in the screen text those checks read) gets its own test.
- What is left off the PDF is **only** what Paul has agreed, and each is marked in the code: the public profile (LinkedIn) (`nopdf` in `schema.js`) and the Claude and OpenAI cost figures. Anything else left out is a bug.
- A PDF that was saved earlier is a picture of the form on the day it was saved: it does not change by itself. To bring one up to date, open the client's file and save the PDF again. Say so when a change would make an earlier PDF look different.
- **The Short Risk Application page (`app/short-risk.html`, added November 2026) follows the same two rules.** Its README section is kept up to date in the same commit as any change to it, and its PDF (`app/js/sr-pdf.js`, drawn from `app/js/sr-schema.js`) mirrors its page: `tests/sr-pdf.test.js` and the browser check `tests/browser/t-sr.js` must pass. What is left off its PDF is only the line of guidance under the Banking heading (`note` in `sr-schema.js`). The `complete-applications-skill` is not connected to it yet (Paul will do that).
- **The Investment Capture Sheet page (`app/capture-sheet.html`, added November 2026) follows the same two rules.** Its README section is kept up to date in the same commit as any change to it, and its **application PDF** (`app/js/cs-pdf.js`, drawn from `app/js/cs-schema.js`) mirrors its pages: `tests/cs-pdf.test.js` and the browser check `tests/browser/t-cs.js` must pass. What is left off the application PDF is only the Risk Tolerance questionnaire's 24 boxes (it has its own PDF, `app/js/cs-rt-pdf.js`, which Paul asked to be saved separately, with the client's signature, place and date left blank to be signed later); its three worked-out result boxes are printed. **The Declaration stays with the application** (Paul's decision), not after the questionnaire.
- **The Risk Tolerance questionnaire is a copy of the Risk Tolerance website's (risktolerance.netlify.app), not a link to it** (Paul agreed this because the form cannot read another website's answers): its statements, scoring, profiles and descriptions are in `app/js/cs-rt-data.js` and the firm's PDF form in `app/assets/rt-form.js`. A change to the website is made there too, and `tests/cs-store.test.js` pins the scoring. The website itself is not part of this repository and is never changed from here.
- **The questionnaire's PDF is saved separately, and meant for the client's "2. Fact Find" folder** (Paul's instruction): the Save window opens in the folder of the Fact Find that was loaded.

## Ids, versions and the change log (set by Paul Kafentzis with the skills chat, always)

- **An id never changes when only its label changes.** Skills and saved files read boxes by id. A box whose **meaning** changes gets a **new id**, and the old id is kept (marked `legacy` in `schema.js`, shown only while a file holds an answer in it) so older files and the skills still work.
- **Every release that changes the form's boxes bumps `version` in `app/js/schema.js`** (in `app/js/sr-schema.js` for the Short Risk Application, in `app/js/cs-schema.js` for the Investment Capture Sheet). A saved file carries it as `schema`.
- **`docs/FORM-CHANGES.md` is kept in the same commit:** dated, one line for each id added, removed or relabelled, each change to a sum or a tax table, and each list column added.
- A renamed label is in the PDF too (it is drawn from the schema; check it).
- **`app/js/worked.js` writes the figures the form works out into the saved file (`worked`) as the PDF prints them.** A new calculated box, list total, area total or change to the instalment protector is added there in the same commit; `tests/worked.test.js` proves the block equals the PDF text. Say in the README and in `docs/FORM-CHANGES.md` when the block changes.

## File naming (set by Paul Kafentzis, do not change unless asked)

- **The Fact Find saved as a PDF and the saved Fact Find file (.json) have the same name**, only the ending differs:
  `Fact Find (Audio to Text) - dd.mm.yy - (Name Surname)`
  for example `Fact Find (Audio to Text) - 04.11.26 - (Sitamah Nathoo)` (then `.pdf` or `.json`).
  The date is the form's *Date completed* with a two-digit year; the name and surname are in round brackets (`(New client)` until a name is entered).
  It is built by `store.pdfFileName()` in `app/js/store.js`; `store.baseFileName()` returns the same name for the .json. Save, Save as, Save PDF and Preview PDF in `app/js/ui.js` use them, also the plain-download fallback.
  Tests in `tests/app.test.js` and `tests/browser/t-settings.js` pin it. (Until 3 November 2026 the PDF said "Voice to Text" and the .json was `Fact Find - dd.mm.yyyy - Name Surname`; Paul changed both.)
- A file that is opened keeps its own name when it is saved again; only the first save of a client's file, and every PDF, get the name above.
